 G2P2   Boilers and pressure vessels 
 
When located in a building, boilers and pressure vessels  must be installed to avoid, during reasonably foreseeable  
conditions, the likelihood of—  
leakage from the vessel which could cause damage to the building; and (a)
rupture or other mechanical damage of the vessel which could cause damage to the building or injury to  (b)
occupants. 
 
 
 
 G2V1   Combustion appliances 
 
Compliance with G2P1(a) and G2P1(b) is verified when—  
components used within an appliance and its installation are constructed from—  (a)
heat-resistant materials for maximum operating temperatures not less than 600 ℃, where the material  (i)
complies with (c); or 
heat-tolerant materials for maximum operating temperatures more than 150℃ and less than 600℃, where (ii)
the material complies with (c); and 
the building elements surrounding the appliance maintain their designed function and material properties inclusive (b)
of a full range of thermal movements when exposed to the heat effects of the appliance; and 
a sample of the material is tested to the maximum operating temperature, specified in (a)(i) or (a)(ii) for a  (c)
minimum of 96 hours; and 
the tested sample, when allowed to cool, is free from—  (d)
visible cracks and fractures; and (i)
visible indication of de-lamination; and (ii)
linear distortion in excess of the equivalent of 10 mm per metre, and (iii)
deterioration of the appearance of any surface finish, when compared to an unheated sample. (iv)
 
 
 
 G2D1   Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions 
 
(1) Where a Deemed-to-Satisfy Solution  is proposed, Performance Requirements  G2P1 and G2P2 are satisfied by  
complying with G2D2 to G2D4. 
(2) Where a Performance Solution  is proposed, the relevant Performance Requirements  must be determined in  
accordance with A2G2(3) and A2G4(3) as applicable.

NSW Part J1 
NT Part J1 
Introduction to this Part 
This Part sets the thermal performance properties of building fabric, the energy efficiency of key energy using equipment 
and the features a building must have to facilitate the future installation of distributed energy resources. 
 
Notes 
For Class 2 buildings, Section J is replaced with Section J of NCC 2022 Amendment 2. 
 
Notes 
In Tasmania, for a Class 2 building and Class 4 part of a building, Section J is replaced with Section J of BCA 2019  
Amendment 1. For all other classes Section J is replaced with Section J of BCA 2022 Amendment 2. 
 
 
 
 J1O1   Objective 
 
The Objective of this Section is to—  
reduce energy consumption and energy peak demand; and (a)
reduce greenhouse gas emissions; and (b)
improve occupant health and amenity. (c)
 
 
 
 J1F1   Energy efficiency 
 
A building must—  
reduce the energy consumption and energy peak demand of key energy using equipment; and (a)
reduce the greenhouse gas emissions that occur as a result of a building’s energy consumption and energy  (b)
source; and 
for a Class 4 part of a building, improve occupant health and amenity by mitigating the impact of extreme hot (c)
and cold weather events and energy blackouts; and 
for other than in a Class 4 part of a building, protect occupant health and amenity by ensuring the building  (d)
envelope assists in the maintenance of acceptable internal conditions while the building is occupied; and 
be able to accommodate the installation of distributed energy resources. (e)
 
 
 
Part J1 Energy efficiency performance requirements
Objectives
Functional Statements
Performance Requirements

===== PAGE 485 =====
Energy efficiency
NCC 2025 Volume One - Building Code of Australia Page 485
 J1P1   Operational energy use and greenhouse gas emissions 
 
A building, other than a Class 4 part of a building, including its services, must have features that—  
facilitate the efficient use of energy and near zero operational greenhouse gas emissions appropriate to—  (a)
the function and use of the building; and (i)
the level of human comfort required for the building use; and (ii)
for the conditioned space, achieve an hourly regulated energy consumption and associated greenhouse gas  (b)
emission, averaged over the annual hours of operation, of not more than—  
for a Class 6 building, 40 kJ/m².hr and 4 g of CO2-e/m2.hr; and (i)
for a Class 5, 7b, 8 or 9a building other than a ward area, or a Class 9b school, 22 kJ/m².hr and 2.2 g of  (ii)
CO2-e/m2.hr; and 
for all other building classifications, 8 kJ/m².hr and 1 g of CO2-e/m2.hr. (iii)
 J1P2   Thermal performance of a Class 4 part of a building 
 
(1) The total heating load of the habitable rooms and conditioned spaces in a Class 4 part of a building must not exceed 
the heating load limit in Specification 44. 
(2) The total cooling load of the habitable rooms and conditioned spaces in a Class 4 part of a building must not exceed 
the cooling load limit in Specification 44. 
(3) The total thermal energy load of the habitable rooms and conditioned spaces in a Class 4 part of a building must not 
exceed the thermal energy load limit in Specification 44. 
 J1P3   Energy usage of a Class 4 part of a building 
 
(1) The energy value of the domestic services of a Class 4 part of a building must not exceed the energy value with—  
a 3-star ducted heat pump, rated under the 2019 GEMS determination, heating all spaces that are provided with (a)
heating; and 
a 3-star ducted heat pump, rated under the 2019 GEMS determination, cooling all spaces that are provided with (b)
cooling; and 
a 5-star instantaneous gas water heater, rated under the 2017 GEMS determination, providing all domestic hot (c)
water; and 
a lighting power density of 4 W/m2 serving all internal spaces that are provided with artificial lighting. (d)
(2) Domestic services, including any associated distribution system and components must, to the degree necessary,  
have features that facilitate the efficient use of energy appropriate to—  
the domestic service and its usage; and (a)
the geographic location of the building; and (b)
the location of the domestic service; and (c)
the energy source. (d)
 J1P4   Renewable energy, energy storage and electric vehicle charging 
 
A building must have features that facilitate the future installation of on-site renewable energy generation and energy  
storage equipment, except where this is already installed to the maximum practicable extent, and electric vehicle charging 
equipment. 
 

===== PAGE 486 =====
Energy efficiency
NCC 2025 Volume One - Building Code of Australia Page 486
 
 
 
 J1V1   NABERS Energy 
 
(1) For a Class 5 building, compliance with J1P1 is verified when—  
a minimum 6-star NABERS Energy for Offices base building Commitment Agreement is obtained; and (a)
the energy model required for (a) demonstrates that the base building’s regulated energy greenhouse gas  (b)
emissions are equivalent to at least 6.6-star when excluding—  
tenant supplementary heating and cooling systems; and (i)
external lighting; and (ii)
carpark services; and (iii)
the energy model required for (a) demonstrates either—  (c)
a thermal comfort level of between a Predicted Mean Vote of -1 to +1 is achieved across not less than 95% (i)
of the floor area of all occupied zones for not less than 98% of the annual hours of operation of the building; 
or 
a dry bulb room temperature is maintained across not less than 95% of the floor area of all occupied zones, (ii)
other than any room containing an indoor swimming pool, for not less than 95% of the annual hours of 
operation between—  
18°C and 25°C for conditioned spaces with transitory occupancy; and (A)
21°C and 24°C for all other conditioned spaces; and (B)
the building complies with the additional requirements in Specification 33. (d)
(2) For a Class 3 building, compliance with J1P1 is verified when—  
a minimum 4-star NABERS Energy for Hotels Commitment Agreement is obtained; and (a)
the operating hours of the services are not less than 12 hours per day in bedrooms, dining rooms and conference (b)
facilities, 24 hours per day in corridors and foyers and 18 hours per day in back-of-house areas; and 
the energy model required for (a) demonstrates that—  (c)
the regulated energy greenhouse gas emissions are equivalent to at least 5.3-stars; and (i)
a dry bulb room temperature is maintained across not less than 95% of the floor area of all occupied zones, (ii)
other than any room containing an indoor swimming pool, for not less than 95% of the annual hours of 
operation between—  
18°C and 25°C for conditioned spaces with transitory occupancy and food preparation areas; and (A)
21°C and 24°C for all other conditioned spaces; and (B)
the space temperature in any indoor swimming pool  chamber is maintained at 2°C above the pool  (iii)
temperature during occupied hours of not less than 12 hours per day; and 
the building complies with the additional requirements in Specification 33. (d)
(3) For a Class 6 shopping centre, compliance with J1P1 is verified when—  
a minimum 6-stars NABERS Energy for Shopping Centres Commitment Agreement is obtained; and (a)
the building has: (b)
an air-conditioned common area of not less than 20% of the gross lettable area; and (i)
a gross lettable area greater than 15 000 m2; and (ii)
the energy model required for (a) demonstrates—  (c)
the regulated energy greenhouse gas emissions of the services covered within the scope of NABERS (i)
Energy for Shopping Centres ratings are equivalent to at least 6.7-stars when—  
excluding carpark services and carpark numbers from NABERS Energy calculation; and (A)
calculating solar photovoltaic export based on the reduced site energy use arising from (A); and (B)
Verification Methods

 J1V2   Green Star 
 
(1) For a Class 3, 5, 6, 7, 8 or 9 building, compliance with J1P1 is verified when—  
the building complies with the simulation requirements, and is registered, for a Green Star Buildings rating; and (a)
the annual greenhouse gas emissions  of the proposed building are less than 90% of the annual greenhouse (b)
gas emissions of the reference building; and 
in the proposed building, a thermal comfort level  of between a Predicted Mean Vote  of -1 to +1 is achieved  (c)
across not less than 95% of the floor area of all occupied zones for not less than 98% of the annual hours of 
operation of the building; and 
the building complies with the additional requirements in Specification 33. (d)
(2) The calculation method used for (1) must comply with ANSI/ASHRAE Standard 140. 
 J1V3   Verification using a reference building 
 
(1) For a Class 3, 5, 6, 7, 8 or 9 building, compliance with J1P1 is verified when—  
it is determined that—  (a)
the annual greenhouse gas emissions  of the proposed building are not more than 97% of the annual (i)
greenhouse gas emissions  of a reference building  when the proposed building is modelled with the  
proposed services; and 
the annual greenhouse gas emissions of the services of the proposed building are not more than the annual (ii)
greenhouse gas emissions of the services of the reference building when the proposed building is modelled 
with the same services as the reference building; and 
in the proposed building—  (b)
if a Class 5 building, a thermal comfort level of between a Predicted Mean Vote of -1 to +1 is achieved  (i)
across not less than 95% of the floor area of all occupied zones for not less than 98% of the annual hours 
of operation of the building; or 
a dry bulb room temperature is maintained across not less than 95% of the floor area of all occupied zones, (ii)
other than any room containing an indoor swimming pool, for not less than 95% of the annual hours of 
operation between—  
18°C and 25°C for conditioned spaces with transitory occupancy; and (A)
21°C and 24°C for all other conditioned spaces; or (B)
in climate zone 1, the 98th percentile zone cooling load across the hours of operation does not exceed 110% (iii)
of the 98th percentile load in the corresponding zone of the reference building, across 95% of the assessable 
floor area; or 
in climate zone 8, the 98 th percentile zone heating load across the hours of operation  does not exceed  (iv)
110% of the 98th percentile load in the corresponding zone of the reference building, across 95% of the  
assessable floor area; or 
in climate zones 2 to 7—  (v)
the 98th percentile zone cooling load across the annual hours of operation does not exceed 120% of (A)
the 98 th percentile load of the corresponding zone of the reference building , across 95% of the

 J1V4   Verification of building envelope sealing 
 
(1) Compliance with J1P1(a) and J1P2 is verified for building envelope sealing when the envelope is sealed at an air  
permeability rate, tested in accordance with Method 1 of AS/NZS ISO 9972, of not more than—  
for a Class 4 part of a building, 10 m³/hr.m² at 50 Pa reference pressure; or (a)
for a Class 5, 6, 8, 9a or 9b building, other than a ward area, in climate zones 1, 7 and 8, 5 m³/hr.m² at 50 Pa  (b)
reference pressure; or 
for a Class 3 or 9c building, or a Class 9a ward area in climate zones 1, 3, 4, 6, 7 and 8, 5 m³/hr.m² at 50 Pa  (c)
reference pressure. 
(2) In a Class 4 part of a building, where an air permeability rate of not more than 5 m3/hr.m2 at 50 Pa reference pressure 
is achieved—  
a mechanical ventilation system must be provided that—  (a)
can be manually overridden; and (i)

NSW Part J3 
NT Part J3 
TAS Part J3 
Introduction to this Part 
This Part contains Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions (elemental) for compliance with Part J1. It sets out provisions for the  
insulation of building fabric and the energy efficiency of domestic services of a Class 4 part of a building. 
 
 
 
 J3D1   Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions 
 
(1) Where a Deemed-to-Satisfy Solution  is proposed, Performance Requirements  J1P1  to J1P4 are satisfied by  
complying with—  
J2D2; and (a)
J3D2 to J3D15; and (b)
J4D2 to J4D7; and (c)
J5D2 to J5D8; and (d)
J6D2 to J6D13; and (e)
J7D2 to J7D9; and (f)
J8D2 to J8D5; and (g)
J9D2 to J9D5. (h)
(2) Where a Performance Solution  is proposed, the relevant Performance Requirements  must be determined in  
accordance with A2G2(3) and A2G4(3) as applicable. 
 J3D2   Application of Part 
 
The Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions of this Part apply to building elements forming the external building fabric and domestic 
services of a Class 4 part of a building. 
 J3D3   Reducing heating and cooling loads of a Class 4 part of a building using 
house energy rating software 
 
(1) The Class 4 part of a building must—  
for reducing the heating or cooling loads—  (a)
collectively achieve an average energy rating of not less than 7 stars, including the separate heating and  (i)
cooling load limits; and 
individually achieve an energy rating of not less than 6 stars, including the separate heating and cooling  (ii)
load limits; and 
for thermal breaks, comply with J3D5; and (b)
Part J3 Elemental provisions for a Class 4 part of a building
Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions

for compensating for a loss of ceiling insulation, other than where the house energy rating software  has  (c)
compensated for a loss of ceiling insulation, comply with Table J3D7w; and 
for general thermal construction, comply with J4D3(1) to J4D3(4); and (d)
for floor edge insulation, comply with J3D10(3), J3D10(5) and J3D10(6); and (e)
for building sealing, comply with Part J5. (f)

 J3D5   Roof thermal breaks of a Class 4 part of a building 
 
(1) A metal-framed flat, skillion or cathedral roof that—  
has metal sheet roofing directly fixed to metal purlins, metal rafters, metal battens or the like; and (a)
does not have a ceiling lining or has a ceiling lining fixed directly to the metal purlins, metal rafters,  metal battens (b)
or the like; and 
has not achieved the required Total R-Value specified in Table J3D7t, calculated using a method that accounts (c)
for the effects of thermal bridging, 
must have a thermal break, consisting of a material with an R-Value of greater than or equal to R0.2, installed within 
the structure. 
(2) The requirements of (1) do not apply to roofs constructed using insulated sandwich panels.

 J3D6   * * * * * 
 
This clause has deliberately been left blank. 
 J3D7   Roofs and ceilings of a Class 4 part of a building 
 
(1) Roof and ceiling insulation must achieve the minimum R-Value—  
in climate zone 1, in accordance with Tables J3D7a, J3D7b and J3D7c as applicable; and (a)
in climate zone 2, in accordance with Tables J3D7d, J3D7e and J3D7f as applicable; and (b)
in climate zone 3, in accordance with Tables J3D7g, J3D7h and J3D7i as applicable; and (c)
in climate zone 4, in accordance with Tables J3D7j, J3D7k and J3D7l as applicable; and (d)
in climate zone 5, in accordance with Tables J3D7m, J3D7n and J3D7o as applicable; and (e)
in climate zone 6—  (f)
R3.5; or (i)
if the roof contains reflective insulation, R3.0; and (ii)
in climate zones 7 and 8, in accordance with Tables J3D7p, J3D7q and J3D7r as applicable. (g)
(2) Reflective insulation installed to comply with (1) must—  
have a surface emittance of not more than 0.05; and (a)
be adjacent to a roof space of not less than 20 mm; and (b)
in climate zones 3 to 8, be downward facing. (c)
(3) The thermal bridging in a metal-framed roof must be addressed as follows—  
for a pitched roof with a horizontal ceiling—  (a)
achieving the Total R-Value in Table J3D7s, calculated using a method that accounts for the effects of  (i)
thermal bridging; or 
increasing the R-Value of the insulation between the ceiling frames by R0.5 more than the R-Value derived (ii)
from (1); or 
adding a continuous ceiling insulation layer with a minimum R-Value of R0.13 above or below the ceiling  (iii)
joists or the bottom chords of the trusses; or 
achieving the required ceiling R-Value derived from (1) by stacking two layers of insulation immediately on (iv)
top of each other, such that the top layer is orientated to cover the ceiling joists or bottom chord of the  
trusses and has an R-Value of at least R0.5; or 
for a flat, skillion or cathedral roof—  (b)
achieving the Total R-Value in Table J3D7t, calculated using a method that accounts for the effects of  (i)
thermal bridging; or 
complying with Table J3D7u. (ii)
(4) Where F8D5(1) applies, continuous insulation placed above the primary insulation layer to mitigate thermal bridging 
must have a vapour permeance of not less than that of the primary insulation layer. 
(5) Where, for operational or safety reasons, the area of ceiling insulation required is reduced, the loss of insulation must 
be compensated for in accordance with Table J3D7w. 
(6) Where the ceiling insulation required by (1) to (5) has an R-Value—  
greater than R3.0 and less than or equal to R4.5, it may be reduced to R3.0 within 450 mm of an external wall; (a)
or 
greater than R4.5, it may be reduced to R3.0 within 450 mm of an external wall, provided all other required (b)
ceiling insulation is increased by R0.5. 
(7) The requirements of (1) to (6) do not apply to roofs constructed using insulated sandwich panels.

R-Values listed are for the labelled, declared R-Value of insulation. (5)

 J3D10   Floors of a Class 4 part of a building 
 
(1) Where a Class 4 part of a building has a concrete floor above an unenclosed carpark, undercroft or the like, underfloor 
insulation must be installed with an R-Value at least—  
in climate zone 2 and climate zones 5 to 8, R2.0; and (a)
in climate zones 3 and 4, R1.5. (b)
(2) Where a Class 4 part of a building has a concrete floor above an enclosed carpark, undercroft or the like, underfloor 
insulation must be installed with an R-Value at least—  
in climate zone 2, R0.5; and (a)
in climate zones 4 and 5, R1.0; and (b)
in climate zone 6, R1.5; and (c)
in climate zones 7 and 8, R2.0. (d)
(3) A concrete slab-on-ground, including a waffle-pod slab, with an in-slab or in-screed heating or cooling system must  
have insulation with an R-Value at least 1.0 installed around the vertical edge of its perimeter. 
(4) A concrete slab-on-ground, other than a waffle-pod slab, must be insulated in accordance with the following: 
In climate zones 6 and 7—  (a)
insulation with an R-Value of at least 0.64 must be installed around the vertical edge of its perimeter; and (i)
insulation with an R-Value of at least 0.64 must be installed underneath the slab. (ii)
In climate zone 8—  (b)
insulation with an R-Value of at least 1.0 must be installed around the vertical edge of its perimeter; and (i)
insulation with an R-Value of at least 2.0 must be installed underneath the slab. (ii)
(5) Insulation required by (3), (4)(a)(i) and (4)(b)(i) must—  
be water resistant; and (a)
be continuous from the adjacent finished ground level—  (b)
to a depth of not less than 300 mm; or (i)
for at least the full depth of the vertical edge of the concrete slab-on-ground. (ii)
(6) The requirements of (3) do not apply to an in-screed heating or cooling system used solely in a bathroom, amenity  
area or the like.

NT Part J4 
Introduction to this Part 
This Part contains Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions for compliance with Part J1. It sets out provisions for the building envelope 
including roofs, ceilings, roof lights, walls, glazing and floors. 
 
 
 
 J4D1   Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions 
 
NSW J4D1(1) 
(1) Where a Deemed-to-Satisfy Solution  is proposed, Performance Requirements  J1P1 to J1P4 are satisfied by  
complying with—  
J2D2; and (a)
J3D2 to J3D15; and (b)
J4D2 to J4D7; and (c)
J5D2 to J5D8; and (d)
J6D2 to J6D13; and (e)
J7D2 to J7D9; and (f)
J8D2 to J8D5; and (g)
J9D2 to J9D5. (h)
(2) Where a Performance Solution  is proposed, the relevant Performance Requirements  must be determined in  
accordance with A2G2(3) and A2G4(3) as applicable. 
 
NSW J4D2 
 J4D2   Application of Part 
 
The Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions of this Part apply to building elements forming the envelope of a Class 3 to 9 building 
other than J4D3(5), J4D4, J4D5, J4D6 and J4D7 which do not apply to a Class 4 part of a building. 
 J4D3   Thermal construction — general  
 
(1) Where required, insulation must comply with AS/NZS 4859.1 and be installed so that it—  
forms a continuous barrier with ceilings, walls, bulkheads, floors, slab-edges or the like that inherently contribute (a)
to the thermal barrier; and 
is continuous below and around box gutters, parapets and the like; and (b)
abuts or overlaps adjoining insulation other than at supporting members such as posts, beams, truss chords,  (c)
rafters, studs, noggings, joists, furring channels, slab edges and the like where the insulation must be against  
the member; and 
does not affect the safe or effective operation of a service or fitting. (d)
Part J4 Building fabric
Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions

===== PAGE 525 =====
Energy efficiency
NCC 2025 Volume One - Building Code of Australia Page 525
(2) Where required, reflective insulation must be installed with—  
the necessary airspace to achieve the required R-Value between a reflective side of the reflective insulation and (a)
a building lining or cladding; and 
the reflective insulation closely fitted against any penetration, door or window opening; and (b)
the reflective insulation adequately supported by framing members; and (c)
each adjoining sheet of roll membrane being—  (d)
overlapped not less than 50 mm; or (i)
taped together. (ii)
(3) Where required, bulk insulation must be installed so that—  
it maintains its position and thickness, other than where it is compressed between cladding and supporting  (a)
members, water pipes, electrical cabling or the like; and 
in a ceiling, where there is no bulk insulation or reflective insulation in the wall beneath, it overlaps the wall by  (b)
not less than 50 mm. 
(4) Roof, ceiling, wall and floor materials, and associated surfaces are deemed to have the thermal properties listed in  
Specification 36. 
(5) The required Total R-Value and Total System U-Value, including allowance for thermal bridging, must be—  
calculated in accordance with AS/NZS 4859.2 for a roof or floor; or (a)
determined in accordance with Specification 37 for wall-glazing construction; or (b)
determined in accordance with Specification 39 or Section 3.5 of CIBSE Guide A for soil or sub-floor spaces. (c)
 J4D4   Roof and ceiling construction 
 
(1) A roof or ceiling must achieve a Total R-Value greater than or equal to—  
for a Class 5, 6, 7, 8 or 9b building or a Class 9a building other than a ward area—  (a)
in climate zones 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5 — R3.7 for a downward direction of heat flow; and  (i)
in climate zone 6 — R3.2 for a downward direction of heat flow; and  (ii)
in climate zone 7 — R3.7 for an upward direction of heat flow; and  (iii)
in climate zone 8 — R4.8 for an upward direction of heat flow.  (iv)
for a Class 3 or 9c building or a Class 9a ward area—  (b)
in climate zones 1, 3, 4 and 6 — R4.8 for a downward direction of heat flow; and  (i)
in climate zone 2 — R4.4 for a downward direction of heat flow; and  (ii)
in climate zone 5 — R3.7 for a downward direction of heat flow; and  (iii)
in climate zones 7 and 8 — R5.3 in an upward direction of heat flow.  (iv)
(2) In climate zones 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 and 7 the upper surface of a roof must have—  
a Total Solar Reflectance greater than 0.55 and a thermal emittance greater than 0.85; or (a)
a Solar Reflectance Index greater than 61; or (b)
a solar absorptance of not more than 0.45 and a thermal emittance of greater than 0.85. (c)
(3) In climate zones  1, 2, 3, 5 and 6 the upper surface of a metal roof, which has outside air intakes, unitary air-
conditioning units or air-cooled chillers mounted on it or less than 2 m above it, must have—  
a Total Solar Reflectance greater than 0.65 and a thermal emittance greater than 0.85; or (a)
a Solar Reflectance Index greater than 80; or (b)
a solar absorptance of not more than 0.35 and a thermal emittance of greater than 0.85. (c)
(4) The requirements of (2) and (3) do not apply to the portion of the upper surface of a roof that is beneath a solar  
photovoltaic panel or a rooftop garden.

Energy efficiency
NCC 2025 Volume One - Building Code of Australia Page 526
 J4D5   Roof lights 
 
Roof lights must have—  
a total area of not more than 5% of the floor area of the room or space served; and (a)
transparent and translucent elements, including any imperforate ceiling diffuser, with a combined performance  (b)
of—  
for Total system SHGC, in accordance with Table J4D5; and (i)
for Total system U-Value, not more than U3.9. (ii)
 Table J4D5: Roof lights – Total system SHGC 
 
Table Notes 
The roof light shaft index is determined by measuring the distance from the centre of the shaft at the roof to the  (1)
centre of the shaft at the ceiling level and dividing it by the average internal dimension of the shaft opening at the  
ceiling level (or the diameter for a circular shaft) in the same units of measurement. 
The area of a roof light is the area of the roof opening that allows light to enter the building. (2)
The total area of roof lights is the combined area for all roof lights serving the room or space. (3)

(5) The solar admittance of externally facing wall-glazing construction, excluding wall-glazing construction which is wholly

for a Class 5, 6, 7, 8 or 9b building or a Class 9a building other than a ward area, the values specified in Table (a)
J4D6c; and 
for a Class 3 or 9c building or a Class 9a ward area, the values specified in Table J4D6d. (b)

NT Part J5 
Introduction to this Part 
This Part contains Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions for compliance with Part J1. It sets out provisions for sealing a building  
to prevent air leakage. The provisions are intended to improve occupant thermal comfort by increasing envelope efficacy 
and reduce the energy consumption of any installed air-conditioning systems. 
 
 
 
 J5D1   Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions 
 
NSW J5D1(1) 
(1) Where a Deemed-to-Satisfy Solution  is proposed, Performance Requirements  J1P1 to J1P4 are satisfied by  
complying with—  
J2D2; and (a)
J3D2 to J3D15; and (b)
J4D2 to J4D7; and (c)
J5D2 to J5D8; and (d)
J6D2 to J6D13; and (e)
J7D2 to J7D9; and (f)
J8D2 to J8D5; and (g)
J9D2 to J9D5. (h)
(2) Where a Performance Solution  is proposed, the relevant Performance Requirements  must be determined in  
accordance with A2G2(3) and A2G4(3) as applicable. 
 
NSW J5D2 
 J5D2   Application of Part 
 
(1) The Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions of this Part apply to—  
elements forming the envelope of a Class 3, 5, 6, 7, 8 or 9 building; and (a)
the external building fabric of a Class 4 part of a building. (b)
(2) Notwithstanding (1), the Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions of this Part do not apply to—  
a building in climate zones 1, 2, 3 or 5 where the only means of air-conditioning is by using an evaporative cooler; (a)
or 
a permanent building opening, in a space where a gas appliance is located, that is necessary for the safe  (b)
operation of a gas appliance; or 
a building or space where the mechanical ventilation required by Part F6 provides sufficient pressurisation to  (c)
prevent infiltration. 
Part J5 Building sealing
Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions

===== PAGE 530 =====
Energy efficiency
NCC 2025 Volume One - Building Code of Australia Page 530
 J5D3   Chimneys and flues 
 
The chimney or flue of an open solid-fuel burning appliance must be provided with a damper or flap that can be closed to 
seal the chimney or flue. 
 J5D4   Roof lights 
 
A roof light must be sealed, or capable of being sealed, with—  
an imperforate ceiling diffuser or the like installed at the ceiling or internal lining level; or (a)
a weatherproof seal; or (b)
a shutter system readily operated either manually, mechanically or electronically by the occupant. (c)
 J5D5   Windows and doors 
 
(1) A door, openable window or the like must be sealed. 
(2) The requirements of (1) do not apply to—  
a window complying with AS 2047; or (a)
a fire door or smoke door; or (b)
a roller shutter door, roller shutter grille or other security door or device installed only for out-of-hours security. (c)
(3) A seal to restrict air infiltration—  
for the bottom edge of a door, must be a draft protection device; and (a)
for the other edges of a door or the edges of an openable window or other such opening, may be a foam or  (b)
rubber compression strip, fibrous seal or the like. 
(4) An entrance to a building, must have an airlock, self-closing door, rapid roller door, revolving door or the like, other  
than—  
where the room containing the entrance has a floor area of not more than 50 m2; or (a)
where a café, restaurant, open front shop or the like has—  (b)
an un-conditioned zone at least 3 m deep between the main entrance, including an open front, and a  (i)
conditioned space; and 
at all other entrances to the café, restaurant, open front shop or the like, self-closing doors; or (ii)
where the entrance is a moveable external wall with the capability to open for the purpose of creating a temporary (c)
unconditioned space; or 
where, for the safety of the building occupants or for the intended use of the facility, it is required to be left open (d)
to a temporary unconditioned space at an early childhood centre, preschool, school, or the like; or 
where the entrance serves a Class 3 sole-occupancy unit or a Class 4 part of a building. (e)
(5) A loading dock entrance, if leading to a conditioned space, must be fitted with a rapid roller door or the like. 
 J5D6   Exhaust fans 
 
An exhaust fan must be fitted with a sealing device such as a self-closing damper or the like unless it is necessary for the 
fan to operate continuously. 
 

===== PAGE 531 =====
Energy efficiency
NCC 2025 Volume One - Building Code of Australia Page 531
 J5D7   Construction of roofs, ceilings, walls and floors 
 
(1) Ceilings, walls, floors and any opening such as a window frame, door frame, roof light frame, ventilation opening  
frame or the like must be constructed to minimise air leakage. 
(2) Construction required by (1) must be—  
enclosed by internal lining systems that are close fitting at ceiling, wall and floor junctions; or (a)
sealed at junctions and penetrations with—  (b)
close fitting architrave, skirting or cornice; or (i)
expanding foam, rubber compressible strip, caulking or the like. (ii)
(3) The requirements of (1) do not apply to openings, grilles, louvres or the like required for smoke hazard management. 
 J5D8   Evaporative coolers 
 
An evaporative cooler must be fitted with a self-closing damper or the like in climate zones 4, 5, 6, 7 and 8.

NCC 2025 Volume One - Building Code of Australia Page 532
  
 
NT Part J6 
Introduction to this Part 
This Part contains Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions for compliance with Part J1. It sets out the provisions for the efficiency  
and control of air-conditioning, space heating and ventilation equipment, the efficiency, sealing and insulation requirements 
for ductwork systems containing fans, and for the efficiency and insulation of pipework and pump systems. 
 
 
 
 J6D1   Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions 
 
NSW J6D1(1) 
(1) Where a Deemed-to-Satisfy Solution  is proposed, Performance Requirements  J1P1 to J1P4 are satisfied by  
complying with—  
J2D2; and (a)
J3D2 to J3D15; and (b)
J4D2 to J4D7; and (c)
J5D2 to J5D8; and (d)
J6D2 to J6D13; and (e)
J7D2 to J7D9; and (f)
J8D2 to J8D5; and (g)
J9D2 to J9D5. (h)
(2) Where a Performance Solution  is proposed, the relevant Performance Requirements  must be determined in  
accordance with A2G2(3) and A2G4(3) as applicable. 
 J6D2   Application of Part 
 
The Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions of this Part do not apply to a Class 8 electricity network substation. 
 J6D3   Air-conditioning system control 
 
(1) An air-conditioning system—  
must be capable of being deactivated when the building or part of a building served by that system is not  (a)
occupied; and 
must not use a single heating coil or a single cooling coil in an air-handling unit to serve more than one air-(b)
conditioning zone where one zone will require heating at the same time another requires cooling; and 
when serving more than one air-conditioning zone or area with different heating or cooling needs, must—  (c)
thermostatically control the temperature of each zone or area; and (i)
not control the temperature by mixing actively heated air and actively cooled air; and (ii)
serve diverse loads through modulating airflow to the maximum extent practicable; and (iii)
limit zone-level reheating of air cooled by equipment upstream of the zone to not more than 7.5°C at any  (iv)

limit zone-level re-cooling of air heated by equipment upstream of the zone to not more than 7.5°C at any  (v)

which provides the required mechanical ventilation, other than in climate zone 1 or where dehumidification control (d)
is needed, must have an outdoor air economy cycle if the total air flow rate of any airside component of an air-

(2) When two or more air-conditioning systems serve the same space they must use control sequences that prevent the 
systems from operating in opposing heating and cooling modes.

 J6D4   Mechanical ventilation system control 
 
NSW J6D4(1) 
(1) A mechanical ventilation system, including one that is part of an air-conditioning system, except where the mechanical 
system serves only a Class 4 part of a building, must be capable of being deactivated when the building or part of  
the building served by that system is not occupied. 
(2) When serving a conditioned space, except in periods when evaporative cooling is being used, a mechanical ventilation 
system must—  
where specified in Table J6D4, have—  (a)
an energy reclaiming system that—  (i)
preconditions outdoor air at a minimum sensible heat transfer effectiveness of 60%; and (A)
is capable of allowing outdoor air to bypass the heat reclaiming system when the conditions do not  (B)
provide a heat reclaim benefit; or 
indirect evaporative cooling that—  (ii)
incorporates an energy reclaiming system meeting the requirements of (i); and (A)
has a minimum humidification efficiency of 85%; or (B)
demand control ventilation in accordance with AS 1668.2 if appropriate to the application; and (iii)
not exceed the minimum outdoor air quantity required by Part F6 by more than 20%, except—  (b)
when additional unconditioned outdoor air is supplied for free cooling; or (i)
where additional mechanical ventilation is needed to balance the required exhaust or process exhaust; or (ii)
where an energy reclaiming system meeting the requirements of (2)(a)(i) preconditions all the outdoor air. (iii)
(3) An exhaust system with an air flow rate of more than 1000 L/s must be capable of stopping the motor when the  
system is not needed, except for an exhaust system in a sole-occupancy unit in a Class 3 or 9c building. 
(4) A time switch must be provided to a mechanical ventilation system with an air flow rate of more than 1000 L/s. 
(5) The time switch provided under (4) must—  
be capable of switching electric power on and off at variable pre-programmed times and on variable pre-(a)
programmed days; and 
have the ability to program public holidays at least 1 year ahead; and (b)

 J6D6   Ductwork insulation 
 
(1) Ductwork and fittings in an air-conditioning system must be provided with insulation—  
complying with AS/NZS 4859.1; and (a)
having an insulation R-Value greater than or equal to—  (b)
for flexible ductwork, 1.0; or (i)
for cushion boxes, that of the connecting ductwork; or (ii)
that specified in Table J6D6. (iii)
(2) Insulation must—  
be protected against the effects of weather and sunlight; and (a)
be installed so that it—  (b)
abuts adjoining insulation to form a continuous barrier; and (i)
maintains its position and thickness, other than at flanges and supports; and (ii)
when conveying cooled air—  (c)
be protected by a vapour barrier on the outside of the insulation; and (i)
where the vapour barrier is a membrane, be installed so that adjoining sheets of the membrane—  (ii)
overlap by at least 50 mm; and (A)
are bonded or taped together. (B)
(3) The requirements of (1) do not apply to—  
ductwork and fittings located within the only or last room served by the system; or (a)
fittings that form part of the interface with the conditioned space; or (b)
return air ductwork in, or passing through, a conditioned space; or (c)
ductwork for outdoor air and exhaust air associated with an air-conditioning system; or (d)
the floor of an in-situ air-handling unit; or (e)
flexible fan connections. (f)
(4) For the purposes of (1), (2) and (3), ductwork and fittings—  
include non-active components of a ductwork system such as cushion boxes; and (a)
exclude active components such as air-handling unit components, fan coil units of air-conditioning or the like. (b)

 J6D7   Ductwork sealing 
 
Ductwork in an air-conditioning system with a capacity of 3000 L/s or greater, not located within the only or last room  
served by the system, must be sealed against air loss in accordance with the duct sealing requirements of AS 4254.1 and 
AS 4254.2 for the static pressure in the system. 
 J6D8   Pump systems 
 
(1) General — Pumps and pipework that form part of an air-conditioning system must either—  
separately comply with (2), (3), (4), (5), (6) and (7); or (a)
achieve a pump motor power per unit of flowrate lower than the pump motor power per unit of flowrate achieved (b)
when applying (2), (3), (4), (5), (6) and (7) together. 
(2) Where the pump motor power input at the duty point is greater than 750 W, a pump must be capable of—  
variable speed operation; and (a)
where serving a distributive system—  (b)
variable volume operation; and (i)
variable pressure operation to the extent permitted by—  (ii)
static head requirements; and (A)
the requirements of downstream equipment. (B)
(3) A distributive system must not have—  
uncontrolled bypasses; or (a)
three port valves. (b)
(4) The requirements of (3)(b) do not apply to a distributive system with a single chiller, including a chiller used for space 
heating, that serves—  
air-conditioning systems with a total co-incident design load of less than 200 kW; or (a)
only one heating coil or one cooling coil. (b)
(5) Circulator pumps — A glandless impeller pump, with a rated hydraulic power output of less than 2.5 kW and that is  
used in closed loop systems must have an energy efficiency index (EEI) not more than 0.27 calculated in accordance 
with European Union Commission Regulation No. 622/2012. 
(6) Other pumps — Pumps that are in accordance with Articles 1 and 2 of European Union Commission Regulation No.  
547/2012 must have a minimum efficiency index (MEI) of 0.4 or more when calculated in accordance with European 
Union Commission Regulation No. 547/2012. 
(7) Pipework — Straight segments of pipework along the index run, forming part of an air-conditioning system—  
in pipework systems that do not have branches and have the same flow rate throughout the entire pipe network, (a)
must achieve an average pressure drop of not more than—  
for constant flow systems, the values nominated in Table J6D8a; or (i)
for variable flow systems, the values nominated in Table J6D8b; or (ii)
in any other pipework system, must achieve an average pressure drop of not more than—  (b)
for constant flow systems, the values nominated in Table J6D8c; or (i)
for variable flow systems, the values nominated in Table J6D8d. (ii)
(8) The requirements of (7) do not apply—  
to valves and fittings; or (a)
where the smallest pipe size compliant with (7) results in a velocity of 0.7 m/s or less at design flow. (b)

 J6D9   Pipework insulation 
 
(1) Piping, vessels, heat exchangers and tanks containing heating or cooling fluid, where the fluid is held at a heated or 
cooled temperature, that are part of an air-conditioning system, other than within appliances covered by MEPS, must 
be provided with insulation—  
complying with AS/NZS 4859.1; and (a)
for piping of heating and cooling fluids, having an insulation R-Value in accordance with Table J6D9a; and (b)
for vessels, heat exchangers or tanks, having an insulation R-Value in accordance with Table J6D9b; and (c)
for refill or pressure relief piping, having an insulation R-Value equal to the required insulation R-Value of the (d)
connected pipe, vessel or tank within 500 mm of the connection. 
(2) Insulation must—  
be protected against the effects of weather and sunlight; and (a)
be able to withstand the temperatures within the piping, vessel, heat exchanger or tank. (b)
(3) Insulation provided to piping, vessels, heat exchangers or tanks containing cooling fluid must be protected by a vapour 
barrier on the outside of the insulation. 
(4) The requirements of (1) and (2) do not apply to piping, vessels or heat exchangers—  
located within the only or last room served by the system and downstream of the control device for the regulation (a)
of heating or cooling service to that room; or 
encased within a concrete slab or panel which is part of a heating or cooling system; or (b)
supplied as an integral part of a chiller, boiler or unitary air-conditioner complying with the requirements of J6D10, (c)
J6D11 and J6D12; or 
inside an air-handling unit, fan-coil unit, or the like. (d)
(5) For the purposes of (1), (2), (3) and (4)—  
heating fluids include heated water, steam and condensate; and (a)
cooling fluids include refrigerant, chilled water, brines and glycol mixtures, but do not include condenser cooling (b)
water.

the annual energy consumption for heating is not more than 15 kWh/m2 of the floor area of the conditioned (ii)
space in climate zones 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5; or 
the in-duct heater complies with J6D3(1)(c)(iv);or (iii)
any combination of (a) to (e). (f)
(2) An electric resistance heater may be used for heating a bathroom if—  
the heating capacity is not more than 1.2 kW; and (a)
the heater has a time switch configured to deactivate the heater after a run-on period not more than 15 minutes (b)
from when there are no occupants in the space served.

be a radiant heater or evaporative cooler; and (a)
be configured to automatically shut down when—  (b)

 J6D13   Heat rejection equipment 
 
(1) The motor rated power of a fan in a cooling tower, closed circuit cooler or evaporative condenser must not exceed  
the allowances in Table J6D13. 
(2) The fan in an air-cooled condenser must have a motor rated power of not more than 42 W for each kW of heat rejected 
from the refrigerant when determined in accordance with AHRI 460. 
(3) A fan with a three-phase motor in heat rejection equipment must be capable of variable speed operation.

a fan in a refrigerant chiller of an air-conditioning system that complies with J6D11; and (a)

refrigerant flow unit that complies with J6D12; and 
a fan in a 4-pipe chiller or air source heat pump water heater that complies with J6D10(8). (c)

NT Part J7 
Introduction to this Part 
This Part contains Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions for compliance with Part J1. It sets out provisions for the design and  
configuration of artificial lighting and power, boiling and chilled water units, lifts and escalators and moving walkways. 
 
 
 
 J7D1   Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions 
 
NSW J7D1(1) 
(1) Where a Deemed-to-Satisfy Solution  is proposed, Performance Requirements  J1P1 to J1P4 are satisfied by  
complying with—  
J2D2; and (a)
J3D2 to J3D15; and (b)
J4D2 to J4D7; and (c)
J5D2 to J5D8; and (d)
J6D2 to J6D13; and (e)
J7D2 to J7D9; and (f)
J8D2 to J8D5; and (g)
J9D2 to J9D5. (h)
(2) Where a Performance Solution  is proposed, the relevant Performance Requirements  must be determined in  
accordance with A2G2(3) and A2G4(3) as applicable. 
 J7D2   Application of Part 
 
J7D3, J7D4 and J7D6(1)(b) do not apply to a Class 8 electricity network substation. 
 J7D3   Artificial lighting 
 
NSW J7D3(1) 
(1) In a Class 4 part of a building—  
the lamp power density or illumination power density of artificial lighting must not exceed the allowance of—  (a)
5 W/m2 within a sole-occupancy unit; and (i)
4 W/m2 on a verandah, balcony or the like attached to a sole-occupancy unit; and (ii)
the illumination power density allowance in (a) may be increased by dividing it by the illumination power density (b)

halogen lamps must be separately switched from fluorescent lamps. (d)

The Room Aspect Ratio of the enclosed space is determined by the formula:  A/(H x C), where—  (3)
A is the area of the enclosed space; and (i)
H is the height of the space measured from the floor to the highest part of the ceiling; and (ii)
C is the perimeter of the enclosed space at floor level. (iii)

density adjustment factor in Table J7D3b and Table J7D3c.

 J7D5   Interior decorative and display lighting 
 
(1) Interior decorative and display lighting, such as for a foyer mural or art display, must be controlled—  
separately from other artificial lighting; and (a)

of areas such as in a museum, art gallery or the like, in which case they may be combined; and 
by a time switch in accordance with Specification 40 where the display lighting exceeds 1 kW. (c)
(2) Window display lighting must be controlled separately from other display lighting.

NT Part J8 
Introduction to this Part 
This Part contains Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions for compliance with Part J1. It sets out provisions for ensuring water  
heaters, swimming pool and spa heaters and pump systems use energy efficiently. 
 
 
 
 J8D1   Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions 
 
NSW J8D1(1) 
(1) Where a Deemed-to-Satisfy Solution  is proposed, Performance Requirements  J1P1 to J1P4 are satisfied by  
complying with—  
J2D2; and (a)
J3D2 to J3D15; and (b)
J4D2 to J4D7; and (c)
J5D2 to J5D8; and (d)
J6D2 to J6D13; and (e)
J7D2 to J7D9; and (f)
J8D2 to J8D5; and (g)
J9D2 to J9D5. (h)
(2) Where a Performance Solution  is proposed, the relevant Performance Requirements  must be determined in  
accordance with A2G2(3) and A2G4(3) as applicable. 
 
NSW J8D2 
 J8D2   Application of Part 
 
The Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions of this Part do not apply to a Class 4 part of a building. 
 J8D3   Heated water supply 
 
(1) Pipework circulating heated water for food preparation and sanitary purposes must be insulated in accordance with  
J6D9, including 500 mm along each branch line. 
(2) Trace-heated pipework carrying heated water for food preparation and sanitary purposes must be insulated in  
accordance with J6D9. 
QLD J8D3(3) 
(3) A building with gas heated water for food preparation and sanitary purposes must be provided with—  
reserved electrical capacity sufficient to support future installation of air-source heat pump water heating  (a)
equipment for food preparation and sanitary purposes assuming a nominal coefficient of performance of 3.25;  
and

 J8D4   Swimming pool heating and pumping 
 
(1) Heating for a swimming pool must be by—  
a solar heater; or (a)
a heater using reclaimed heat from another process such as reject heat from a refrigeration plant; or (b)
a geothermal heater; or (c)
a gas heater that achieves a minimum gross thermal efficiency of 90%; or (d)
a heat pump; or (e)
a combination of (a) to (e). (f)
(2) Where some or all of the heating required by (1) is by a gas heater or a heat pump, the swimming pool must have—  
a cover with a minimum R-Value of 0.05; and (a)
a time switch to control the operation of the heater. (b)
(3) A time switch must be provided to control the operation of a circulation pump for a swimming pool. 
(4) Where required, a time switch must be capable of switching electric power on and off at variable pre-programmed  
times and on variable pre-programmed days. 
(5) Pipework carrying heated or chilled water for a swimming pool must comply with the insulation requirements of J6D9. 
QLD J8D4(6) 
(6) A building with gas heated water for a swimming pool must be provided with—  
reserved electrical capacity sufficient to support future installation of air-source heat pump water heating  (a)
equipment for the swimming pool assuming a nominal coefficient of performance of 3.25; and 
reserved space within the main electrical distribution board comprising at least one empty three-phase circuit  (b)
breaker slot and 4 DIN rail spaces per 35 kW of water heating capacity, labelled to indicate reservation for future 
installation of water heating equipment for the swimming pool; and

accordance with (a); and 
reserved plant area with—  (d)
space to accommodate heating plant of 7 m² plus 0.04 m²/kW of heating capacity; and (i)
sufficient ventilation openings to achieve airflow of 115 L/s per kW of heating capacity with no recirculation (ii)
of air; and 
a hot water circulation system that operates at a peak temperature of less than 50°C. (e)
QLD J8D4(7) 
(7) The capacity and space required by (6) must be sufficient to serve the same design load used to specify any gas-
powered swimming pool water heating equipment. 
(8) For the purpose of J8D4, a swimming pool does not include a spa pool.

 J8D5   Spa pool heating and pumping 
 
(1) Heating for a spa pool that shares a water recirculation system with a swimming pool must be by—  
a solar heater; or (a)
a heater using reclaimed heat from another process such as reject heat from a refrigeration plant; or (b)
a geothermal heater; or (c)
a gas heater that achieves a minimum gross thermal efficiency of 90%; or (d)
a heat pump; or (e)
a combination of (a) to (e). (f)

(3) A time switch must be provided to control the operation of a circulation pump for a spa pool having a capacity of 680 
L or more. 
(4) Where required, a time switch must be capable of switching electric power on and off at variable pre-programmed  
times and on variable pre-programmed days. 
(5) Pipework carrying heated or chilled water for a spa pool must comply with the insulation requirements of J6D9. 
QLD J8D5(6)

reserved electrical capacity sufficient to support future installation of air-source heat pump water heating  (a)

breaker slot and 4 DIN rail spaces per 35 kW of water heating capacity, labelled to indicate reservation for future 
installation of water heating equipment for the spa pool; and

space to accommodate heating plant of 7 m² plus 0.04 m²/kW of heating capacity; and (i)
sufficient ventilation openings to achieve airflow of 115 L/s per kW of heating capacity, with no recirculation (ii)
of air; and 
a hot water circulation system and associated coils and heat exchangers serving the spa pool, designed to  (e)
operate a peak temperature of less than 50°C. 
QLD J8D5(7) 
(7) The capacity and space required by (6) must be sufficient to serve the same design load used to specify any gas-
powered spa pool water heating equipment.

NT Part J9 
Introduction to this Part 
This Part contains Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions  for compliance with Part J1. It sets out provisions that enable the  
monitoring of energy use (other than for billing purposes) and facilitate easy retrofit of renewable energy and electric  
vehicle charging equipment. 
 
 
 
 J9D1   Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions 
 
NSW J9D1(1)

complying with—  
J2D2; and (a)
J3D2 to J3D15; and (b)
J4D2 to J4D7; and (c)

J6D2 to J6D13; and (e)
J7D2 to J7D9; and (f)
J8D2 to J8D5; and (g)
J9D2 to J9D5. (h)
(2) Where a Performance Solution  is proposed, the relevant Performance Requirements  must be determined in  
accordance with A2G2(3) and A2G4(3) as applicable. 
 
NSW J9D2 
 J9D2   Application of Part 
 
The Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions of this Part do not apply—  
within a Class 4 part of a building; or (a)
to a Class 8 electricity network substation. (b)
 J9D3   Facilities for energy monitoring 
 
(1) A building or sole-occupancy unit with a floor area of more than 500 m2 must have energy meters configured to record 
the time-of-use consumption of gas and electricity. 
(2) A building with a floor area of more than 2 500 m2 must have energy meters configured to enable individual time-of-
use energy data recording, in accordance with (3), of—  
air-conditioning plant including, where appropriate, heating plant, cooling plant and air handling fans; and (a)
artificial lighting; and (b)

central hot water supply; and (d)

on-site renewable energy equipment; and (f)
on-site electric vehicle charging equipment; and (g)
on-site battery systems; and (h)
other ancillary plant. (i)
(3) Energy meters required by (2) must be interlinked by a communication system that collates the time-of-use energy

allow scheduling, supervisory level setpoint override and modulation set-back of the air-conditioning  and  (e)

incorporate monitoring and verification functionality with the capability to—  (f)
provide monitoring and verification of a change in energy use in accordance with the International  (i)
Performance Measurement and Verification Protocol Options B and C; and 
automatically determine an energy performance baseline from historical building data and weather data;  (ii)
and 
compare building performance against the baseline, both numerically and graphically; and (iii)
 

===== PAGE 562 =====
Energy efficiency
NCC 2025 Volume One - Building Code of Australia Page 562
provide forecasts of the building’s renewable energy production. (iv)
(7) The requirements of (5) and (6) do not apply to services that consume less than 5% of the building’s total annual  
energy consumption. 
 
Notes 
An Environmental Management Information System that complies with J9D3(5) and (6) and records data as required 
by J9D3(2)(a) to (i) satisfies the requirement for ‘energy meters’ in J9D3(2). 
 
 J9D4   Facilities for electric vehicle charging equipment 
 
(1) Subject to (2), a carpark associated with a Class 3, 5, 6, 7b, 8 or 9 building must be provided with electrical distribution 
boards dedicated to electric vehicle charging—  
in accordance with Table J9D4 in each storey of the carpark; and (a)
labelled to indicate use for electric vehicle charging equipment. (b)
(2) Electrical distribution boards dedicated to serving electric vehicle charging in a carpark must—  
be fitted with a charging control system with the ability to manage and schedule charging of electric vehicles in (a)
response to total building demand; and 
when associated with a Class 5 to 9 building, have capacity for each circuit to support an electric vehicle charger (b)
able to deliver a minimum of 12 kWh from 9:00 am to 5:00 pm daily; and 
when associated with a Class 3 building, have capacity for each circuit to support an electric vehicle charger  (c)
able to deliver a minimum of 48 kWh from 11:00 pm to 7:00 am daily; and 
be sized to support the future installation of a 7 kW (32 A) type 2 electric vehicle charger in—  (d)
10% of car parking spaces associated with a Class 5 or 6 building; or (i)
20% of car parking spaces associated with a Class 3, 7b, 8 or 9 building; and (ii)
contain space of at least 36 mm width of DIN rail per outgoing circuit for individual sub-circuit for individual sub-(e)
circuit electricity metering to record electricity use of electric vehicle charging equipment; and 
be labelled to indicate the use of the space required by (f) is for the future installation of metering equipment. (f)
 
Limitations 
J9D4 does not apply to a stand alone Class 7a building.

 J9D5   Facilities for solar photovoltaic and battery systems 
 
(1) The main electrical switchboard of a building must contain at least 2 empty three-phrase circuit breaker slots and 4  
DIN rail spaces labelled to indicate the use of each space for a battery system. 
(2) A building must be provided with an on-site solar photovoltaic system—  
that covers 100% of the roof area, excluding areas—  (a)
shaded for more than 25% of annual daylight hours; or (i)
where the roof pitch exceeds 45° relative to horizontal; or (ii)
that are awnings; or (iii)
that are car parks; or (iv)
that are roof gardens; or (v)
that are terraces or other rooftop areas designated for occupation; or (vi)
used for roof lights or translucent roofing for natural lighting or the like; or (vii)
designated as a trafficable area, access for height safety systems or plant maintenance access or a  (viii)
required path of travel to an exit; or 
used for plant equipment, services installations or the like; or (ix)
above exhaust air flows from heat rejection equipment or the like; or (x)
where provision of solar photovoltaic panel is impractical on account of panel size or mounting system  (xi)
limitations; or 
with a peak system output rating per m 2 of building conditioned space not less than those specified in Table (b)
J9D5a. 
(3) In addition to (2), a building where gas is used for building services must be provided with further on-site photovoltaic 
system peak output capacity not less than the value specified in Table J9D5b. 
(4) If the on-site solar photovoltaic system in (3) is placed on a building roof, the panels must—  
not be shaded for more than 25% of daylight hours; and (a)
not have a pitch of more than 45°. (b)
 
Limitations 
The requirements of J9D5(2) do not apply to a building—  (1)
where 100% of the effective roof area is shaded for more than 70% of annual daylight hours; or (a)
provided with an on-site renewable energy generation system with a generation capacity at least equivalent to (b)
a solar photovoltaic system described under J9D5(2). 
The requirements of J9D5(1) do not apply to a building with battery systems installed. (2)

Explanatory Information 
Where compliance with J9D5 leads to the installation of an on-site solar photovoltaic system, existing network connection 
rules, governed by the Distribution Network Service Provider (DNSP), will apply. Depending on the size of the system 
and the amount of PV already installed in the local area, the DNSP may require a building to have certain features that 
help maintain local electricity grid stability. These could include options for a dynamic grid connection, demand response 
capability as per J9D3(6) for key energy using equipment, or on-site energy storage.

 S34C1   Scope 
 
This Specification contains the required modelling parameters for J1V3. 
 S34C2   Reference building 
 
The annual greenhouse gas emissions must be calculated for the reference building in accordance with the following: 
The reference building must—  (a)
subject to (b), (c) and (d), comply with Deemed-to-Satisfy Provisions in Parts J4 to J8; and (i)
have the minimum amount of mechanical ventilation required by Part F6; and (ii)
be provided with a solar photovoltaic system that complies with—  (iii)
J9D5(2)(a), J9D5(3) and J9D5(4) with PV panels modelled as parallel to the roof and assuming an  (A)
average solar photovoltaic panel power rating of 225 W/m2; or 
J9D5(2)(b), J9D5(3) and J9D5(4). (B)
The external walls must have a solar absorptance of 0.6. (b)
The U-Value of the wall-glazing construction must be calculated according to S37C3. (c)
The solar admittance of the wall-glazing construction must be calculated according to S37C5. (d)
Where J1V3(1)(b)(i) or J1V3(1)(b)(ii) applies, the air-conditioning must—  (e)
for 95% of the annual hours of operation, achieve dry bulb temperatures between—  (i)
18°C to 25°C for conditioned spaces with transitory occupancy; and (A)
subject to (ii), 21°C to 24°C in all other conditioned spaces; and (B)
if the proposed building has no mechanically provided cooling or has mixed mode cooling, have the same (ii)
method of control and control set points for non-mechanical cooling as the proposed building. 
The infiltration rate in each zone must be—  (f)
0.7 air changes per hour throughout all zones when there is no mechanically supplied outdoor air; and (i)
0.35 air changes per hour throughout all zones at all other times. (ii)
The artificial lighting must achieve the required maximum illumination power density in Part J7 without applying (g)
the control device adjustment factors. 
Minimum Energy Performance Standards must be applied to services not covered by Parts J6 to J8. (h)

 S36C1   Scope 
 
This Specification lists the thermal properties of some common construction materials. 
 S36C2   Construction Deemed-to-Satisfy 
 
(1) Tables S36C2a, S36C2b, S36C2c, S36C2d and S36C2e list the thermal conductivity considered to be achieved by  
some common construction materials. 
(2) For the purposes of Tables S36C2a, S36C2b, S36C2c, S36C2d and S36C2e: 
For materials which incorporate cores or hollows in regular patterns (such as cored brickwork, hollow blockwork (a)
and cored floor or wall panels), the tabulated material densities and thermal conductivities are based on the  
gross density (mass divided by external dimensions). 
The R-Value of a material is determined by dividing the thickness of the material in metres by the thermal  (b)
conductivity in Wm-1K-1. 
(3) Tables S36C2f, S36C2g, S36C2h, S36C2i, S36C2j, S36C2k, S36C2l and S36C2m list the R-Value considered to be 
achieved by air films and airspaces. 
(4) For an envelope that contains a ventilated airspace, the Total R-Value of the building fabric must be reduced based 
on the area of ventilation openings in accordance with clause 6.3 of AS/NZS 4859.2. 
(5) The requirements of (4) do not apply to a roof with an airspace greater than 300 mm or a pitched roof greater than  
5° with a horizontal ceiling.

 Table S36C2h: Typical R-Values for airspaces: non-reflective non-ventilated 
 
Table Notes 
Linear interpolation may be used to calculate the R-Value of the airspace in a roof with an intermediate pitch. (1)
A non-ventilated airspace in a roof is one with continuous cover, such as metal or sarked tiles, and no specific  (2)
provision for ventilation. 
R-Values are calculated using AS/NZS 4859.2 based on the following: (3)
summer temperatures of 24°C internally and 36°C externally for heat transfer down; and (a)
winter temperatures of 18°C internally and 12°C externally for heat transfer up; and (b)
average of summer and winter results for horizontal heat transfer (e.g. in walls). (c)
A non-ventilated airspace in a wall is one where there is no express provision for airflow through it and openings  (4)
to the external environment do not exceed more than 500 mm2 per metre of length in the horizontal direction. 
A non-ventilated airspace in a wall for the purposes of Note (4) includes a wall with drainage openings or weepholes (5)
that are open vertical joints in the outer leaf of a cavity masonry wall, which are not regarded as ventilation openings.

 S37C1   Scope 
 
This specification describes the methods of calculating the U-Value and solar admittance of a wall-glazing construction. 
 S37C2   General 
 
For determining the aspect of a wall-glazing construction—  
the northern aspect is within 45° of true north; and (a)
the southern aspect is within 45° of true south; and (b)
the eastern aspect is within 45° of true east; and (c)
the western aspect is within 45° of true west. (d)
 S37C3   U-Value —  Method 1 (Single Aspect) 
 
(1) For the purposes of this method, a wall-glazing construction only includes the walls and glazing facing a single aspect. 
(2) The Total System U-Value of the wall component of a wall-glazing construction must be calculated as the inverse of 
the Total R-Value, including allowance for thermal bridging, in accordance with—  
AS/NZS 4859.2; or (a)
Specification 38 for spandrel panels. (b)
(3) The Total System U-Value of a wall-glazing construction must be calculated as the area-weighted average of the  
Total System U-Value of each of the components of the wall-glazing construction. 
 S37C4   U-Value —  Method 2 (Multiple Aspects) 
 
(1) For the purposes of this method, a wall-glazing construction  only includes the walls and glazing facing multiple  
aspects. 
(2) The Total System U-Value of the wall component of a wall-glazing construction must be calculated as the inverse of 
the Total R-Value, including allowance for thermal bridging, in accordance with—  
AS/NZS 4859.2; or (a)
Specification 38 for spandrel panels. (b)
(3) The Total System U-Value of a wall-glazing construction must be calculated as the area-weighted average of the  
Total System U-Value of each of the components of the wall-glazing construction. 
 S37C5   Solar admittance —  Method 1 (Single Aspect) 
 
(1) The solar admittance of a wall-glazing construction must be calculated in accordance with the following formula:

 S38C1   Scope 
 
This Specification describes methods of determining the thermal performance of spandrel panels. 
 S38C2   Spandrel panel R-Value: Calculation method 1 
 
Spandrel panels are deemed to have the thermal properties nominated in Table S38C2, where—  
Configuration 1 consists of—  (a)
a thermally unbroken (bridged) frame; and (i)
a centre of spandrel panel consisting of—  (ii)
a single-glazed opaque or clear face; and (A)
a 100 mm air gap; and (B)
a 3 mm aluminium, 0.8 mm galvanised steel or zinc back pan; and (C)
Configuration 2 consists of—  (b)
a thermally unbroken (bridged) frame; and (i)
a centre of spandrel panel consisting of—  (ii)
a double-glazed opaque face; and (A)
a 50 mm air gap; and (B)
a 3 mm aluminium, 0.8 mm galvanised steel or zinc back pan; and (C)
Configuration 3 consists of—  (c)
a thermally broken (unbridged) frame; and (i)
a centre of spandrel panel consisting of—  (ii)
a double-glazed clear face; and (A)
a 50 mm air gap; and (B)
a 3 mm aluminium, 0.8 mm galvanised steel or zinc back pan; and (C)
Configuration 4 consists of—  (d)
a thermally broken (unbridged) frame; and (i)
a centre of spandrel panel consisting of—  (ii)
a double-glazed low-e clear face; and (A)
a 50 mm air gap; and (B)
a 3 mm aluminium, 0.8 mm galvanised steel or zinc back pan. (C)

 S40C1   Scope 
 
This Specification contains the requirements for lighting and power control devices including manual switches, time  
switches, demand-operated controls and daylight control devices. 
 S40C2   Manual switch 
 
An artificial lighting manual switch must be located in a visible and easily accessed position—  
in the room or space being switched; or (a)
in an adjacent room or space from where at least 90% of the lighting being controlled is visible. (b)
 
 S40C3   Time switch 
 
(1) A time switch must—  
be capable of switching on and off electric power at variable pre-programmed times and on variable pre-(a)
programmed days; and 
have the ability to program public holidays at least 1 year ahead. (b)
(2) A time switch for internal lighting must be capable of being overridden by—  
a demand-operated control; or (a)
a manual switch. (b)
(3) A time switch for external lighting must be capable of being configured to limit the period the system is switched on  
to between 30 minutes before sunset and 30 minutes after sunrise. 
(4) A time switch for boiling water or chilled water storage units must be capable of being overridden for a maximum  
period of 2 hours outside normal operating hours. 
 S40C4   Demand-operated control 
 
(1) A demand-operated control must—  
activate artificial lighting upon detecting the presence or movement of an occupant; and (a)
deactivate artificial lighting immediately or after a run-on period not more than 15 minutes after the space is  (b)
vacated. 
(2) A demand-operated control in a fire-isolated stairway , fire-isolated passageway  or fire-isolated ramp  must be  
configured so that a minimum of 30% of the full design illumination is provided when deactivation required by (1)(b)  
has occurred. 
 
Notes 
For the purposes of S40C4, demand-operated controls may detect the presence or movement of an occupant via a  
motion detector, key card, security system or the like. 
 
Specification 40 Lighting and power control devices

===== PAGE 599 =====
Energy efficiency
NCC 2025 Volume One - Building Code of Australia Page 599
 S40C5   Daylight sensor and dynamic lighting control device 
 
A daylight sensor and dynamic control device for artificial lighting must, in response to the measured level of available  
daylight in the range 50–1000 lux—  
for high pressure discharge lights, switch lights on and off with a programmable delay and switching differential; (a)
and 
for all other lights, dim continuously or step lighting output down in not less than 4 steps so that the lowest power (b)
setting is less than 50% of full power.

Energy efficiency
NCC 2025 Volume One - Building Code of Australia Page 600

 S44C1   Scope 
 
This Specification contains the method of calculating the heating load limit, cooling load limit and thermal energy load limit 
for compliance with J1P2 and H6P1. 
 S44C2   Heating load limit 
 
The heating load limit of a space, measured in MJ/m2.annum, is equal to the greater of—  
4; and (a)
 , where—  (b)
  = the total annual heating degree hours of the building location; and (i)
  = the area adjustment factor for the heating load limit, determined in accordance with Table S44C2. (ii)

Explanatory Information

 S44C3   Cooling load limit

 S46C1   Scope 
 
This Specification contains the method of calculating fan power ratio for the purpose of assessing compliance with J6D5. 
 S46C2   Determination of peak efficiency and fan power ratio 
 
(1) The efficiency of a selected fan is the product of its impeller efficiency (including any casing), motor efficiency and  
the integrated motor control equipment multiplier in (2). 
(2) The value of the integrated motor control equipment multiplier—  
where the fan motor is not capable of variable speed operation or has an external variable speed control device, (a)
is 1; or 
where the fan motor has integrated variable speed control capability and the motor input power at peak efficiency (b)
point is less than 5 kW,  , where—  
  = the motor input power of the fan (kW) at peak efficiency point; and (i)
  = natural logarithm; or (ii)
where the fan motor has integrated variable speed control capability and the motor input power at peak efficiency (c)
point is greater than or equal to 5 KW, is 1.04. 
(3) The fan power ratio of an individual fan must be calculated in accordance with the following formula: 
  
where—  
  = the fan power ratio of the fan; and (a)
  = the efficiency calculated using the equation at J6D5(2) or J6D5(3) as applicable, with electrical input power  (b)
, as determined at (5); and 
  = the static efficiency of the selected fan at the duty point for installation type A or C, or the total efficiency  (c)
at the duty point for installation type B or D. 
(4) The average fan power ratio of multiple fans must be calculated in accordance with the following formula: 
  
where—  
  = the average fan power ratio of multiple fans; and (a)
  = the electrical input power of each selected fan at duty point, calculated in accordance with (5); and (b)
  = the electrical input power of each reference fan at duty point, calculated in accordance with (6). (c)
(5)   must be calculated in accordance with the following:

 S47C1   Scope 
 
This Specification contains the method to calculate the climate specific part load value for a connected group of chillers  
that serves a common load, for the purposes of assessing compliance with J6D11. 
 S47C2   Calculation of climate specific part load value for chillers 
 
(1) The climate specific part load value  for a group of one or more chillers must be calculated in accordance with the  
following formula: 
  
where—  
 = climate specific part load value; and (a)
  = the weighted average energy efficiency ratio of the chillers that operate to provide n% of the design  (a)
cooling load under the staging strategy to be deployed, determined in accordance with (2) and (3); and 
  = load weighting factors specified in—  (b)
Table S47C2a for chillers serving a Class 5, 6, 7, 8 or 9b building or a Class 9a building other than a ward (i)
area; or 
Table S47C2b for chillers serving a Class 3 or 9c building or a Class 9a ward area. (ii)
(2)   is determined as the weighted average energy efficiency ratio of the chillers operating to meet n% of the design 
cooling load under the staging strategy to be deployed, allowing for the part load efficiencies at—  
a chilled water leaving temperature of 7°C; and (a)
a chilled water flow rate that corresponds to a 6°C difference between entering and leaving temperatures at the (b)
rated cooling capacity, when determined in accordance with AHRI 551/591; and 
for water-cooled chillers—  (c)
a condenser water entering temperature that is determined by adding the design approach temperature to (i)
the outside wet bulb temperature listed in—  
Table S47C2c for chillers serving a Class 5, 6, 7, 8 or 9b building or a Class 9a building other than a  (A)
ward area; or 
Table S47C2d for chillers serving a Class 3 or 9c building or a Class 9a ward area; and (B)
a condenser water flow rate that corresponds to a 6°C difference between entering and leaving temperature (ii)
at the rated cooling capacity, when determined in accordance with AHRI 551/591; and 
for air-cooled chillers, the outside dry bulb temperature listed in—  (d)
Table S47C2e for chillers serving a Class 5, 6, 7, 8 or 9b building or a Class 9a building other than a ward (i)
area; or 
Table S47C2f for a Class 3 or 9c building or a Class 9a ward area. (ii)
(3) The   values in (2) must be determined using test measurements and calculations performed in accordance with 
the procedures of AHRI 551/591.

 S48C1   Scope 
 
This Specification contains the requirements for comparing sensible heating and cooling loads for each assessable zone 
in the proposed building with the corresponding zones in the reference building. 
 S48C2   Comparison of zone loads in proposed building and reference building 
 
(1) The zone load is the sensible heating or cooling effect required to maintain the temperature in each zone within the  
range specified in S34C3(8). 
(2) The loads for each zone in the proposed building and the reference building must be calculated in accordance with  
the modelling parameters of Specification 34. 
(3) A zone of the proposed building is assessable under J1V3(1)(b)(iii) to (v) if it has—  
a 98th percentile heating load of at least 10 W/m2: or (a)
a 98th percentile cooling load of at least 40 W/m2.(b)
Specification 48 Comparison of zone loads in proposed building and 
reference building
